# ACMS Feature Delivery + Safe Deployment + Automated Rollback Plan

**Date:** 2026-09-26  
**Repository:** `startupteams/acms-project-framework`  
**Environment:** MARION-IA-USA internal ACMS deployment  
**Current UI:** ACMS v0.3.0 internal web UI with LLDAP login, health/database status, and registered-agent view  
**Primary goal:** Turn the live ACMS installation into a progressively useful management system while ensuring every deployment can be safely rolled back by an AI agent if a feature breaks the live service.

## 1. Current state

The live ACMS is operational:

- internal HTTPS UI is reachable;
- LLDAP authentication works;
- the user can log in as an ACMS Administrator;
- application health is OK;
- PostgreSQL health is OK;
- at least one internal agent is registered;
- the UI is intentionally still limited.

The repository has advanced beyond the first UI scaffold. Current `main` includes the work-orchestration backend merged through PR #4, including:

- hierarchical Work Items;
- one active primary assignment per agent;
- execution-task records;
- background-routine inventory;
- Markdown handoff records;
- work/assignment/task/routine/handoff API endpoints.

The current UI does not yet expose those features.

The current deployment tooling already contains Docker Compose, PostgreSQL, nginx, Alembic, `deploy/deploy.sh`, `deploy/update.sh`, `deploy/smoke-test.sh`, and a manual Git-commit rollback procedure.

However, the existing rollback path is not yet strong enough for autonomous recovery from a bad database migration because it does **not** create a pre-deployment database backup and does **not** automatically restore the previous release when post-deploy validation fails.

That must be corrected before rapid feature deployment begins.

## 2. Development strategy: vertical usable slices

From this point forward, prefer **vertical slices** instead of implementing large backend areas that remain invisible for many PRs.

A feature PR should normally include all layers required to make one human-visible capability usable:

```text
database/domain
      +
service/API
      +
authorization
      +
web UI
      +
tests
      +
deployment compatibility
      +
documentation
```

Small PRs are preferred. The live system should become more useful after nearly every successful deployment.

## 3. Deployment authority model

Use this workflow:

```text
feature branch
   ↓
pull request
   ↓
human review
   ↓
merge to main
   ↓
human authorizes deployment
   ↓
AI agent deploys approved main commit
   ↓
automated validation
   ↓
SUCCESS → release stays live
   ↓
FAILURE → AI agent automatically rolls back
```

A deployment is a human-gated action.

Once a human explicitly authorizes deployment of a specific merged release, **rollback to the immediately previous known-good release is pre-authorized as a safety action** if validation fails.

The rollback agent does not need to wait for a second human approval merely to return the service to the exact pre-deployment state.

The agent must stop and request human direction if it cannot prove that the previous state can be safely restored.

## 4. PR 0 — harden deployment and rollback first

Create a focused infrastructure PR before more live feature work.

Recommended branch:

`ops/ACMS-safe-release-rollback`

This PR should not add product features.

### Required deployment metadata

Every deployment must record:

- previous Git SHA;
- target Git SHA;
- semantic ACMS version;
- Docker image tag/digest;
- pre-deploy Alembic revision;
- post-deploy Alembic revision;
- deployment start/end timestamp;
- database backup path;
- database backup checksum;
- deployment result;
- rollback result when applicable.

Store operational release records outside Git, for example:

```text
/opt/acms/releases/
├── current
├── previous
├── history.jsonl
└── backups/
    ├── 20260926T..._<git-sha>.dump
    └── ...
```

Do not put secrets in release metadata.

### Expose build identity

The UI currently showing only `0.3.0` is not enough to identify deployed code.

Update `/version` and the System UI to show at least:

```text
ACMS version: 0.3.0
Git SHA:      eab493f...
Build time:   ...
DB revision:  ...
```

A Git SHA is the authoritative deployed code identity.

### Tag application images by Git SHA

Each built release should also be tagged with the Git SHA, for example:

`acms-app:eab493fe218b`

Keep at least the current and previous known-good application images locally.

## 5. Pre-deployment database backup

Before every production deployment that may run migrations, create a PostgreSQL backup.

Recommended:

```bash
pg_dump -Fc
```

Store backups under:

`/opt/acms/releases/backups/`

The deployment agent must:

1. create the backup;
2. verify the command succeeded;
3. verify the file is non-empty;
4. calculate a SHA-256 checksum;
5. record the checksum in deployment metadata;
6. refuse migration if backup validation fails.

Suggested retention:

- keep at least the most recent 10 deployment backups;
- never delete the only backup associated with the current or previous known-good release;
- PBS/Proxmox backups remain the disaster-recovery layer.

## 6. Deployment maintenance window

To make automatic database rollback safe, prevent new ACMS writes between the pre-deployment backup and successful completion of post-deploy validation.

For the MVP, use a short maintenance window:

1. put the UI into maintenance mode or stop ACMS application writes;
2. create the pre-deploy PostgreSQL backup;
3. build/select the target image;
4. run Alembic migrations;
5. start the target application;
6. run validation;
7. if validation succeeds, exit maintenance mode;
8. if validation fails, keep maintenance mode enabled and execute rollback.

A brief internal outage is preferable to complex zero-downtime deployment at this stage.

## 7. Safe release transaction

Enhance `deploy/update.sh` or add:

`deploy/release.sh`

### Phase A — preflight

Verify:

- target commit is part of approved `origin/main`;
- production working tree has no uncommitted changes;
- PostgreSQL is healthy;
- current ACMS health is good before deployment;
- disk space is sufficient;
- target image builds successfully;
- migration graph is valid;
- required secrets exist;
- previous release metadata can be determined.

If preflight fails, do not modify the live release.

### Phase B — preserve old state

Record old Git SHA, old image, and old Alembic revision.

Enter maintenance mode / stop writes.

Create and validate PostgreSQL backup.

### Phase C — deploy

- checkout approved target SHA;
- build/tag target image;
- run `alembic upgrade head`;
- start target app;
- reload proxy if necessary.

### Phase D — validate

If all required validation passes:

- mark release successful;
- update `current` and `previous`;
- exit maintenance mode;
- preserve pre-deploy backup;
- write deployment handoff.

If validation fails:

**automatically invoke rollback.**

## 8. Rollback strategy

Use the lowest rollback level that reliably returns ACMS to a known-good state.

### Level 0 — service restart

Use when code/schema did not change and the failure appears transient.

Action:

- restart failed service/container;
- rerun smoke tests.

### Level 1 — application-only rollback

Use when:

- the application release is broken;
- migration did not run, or the schema is known to remain backward-compatible;
- there is no evidence of data corruption.

Action:

1. stop failed application;
2. switch to previous Git SHA / previous Docker image;
3. start previous app;
4. run smoke tests;
5. verify LLDAP login;
6. verify registered agents/work data.

Do not run Alembic downgrade automatically.

### Level 2 — application + database restore

Use when:

- failed release ran a migration;
- compatibility with previous app cannot be proven;
- migration partially failed;
- schema/data state is suspicious;
- post-deploy validation fails after migration.

Because deployment is under maintenance mode, restoring the pre-deploy backup should return the system to the exact previous state.

Sequence:

1. keep ACMS stopped / maintenance active;
2. verify pre-deploy dump exists;
3. verify checksum;
4. record failed Git SHA and DB revision if readable;
5. terminate active ACMS database connections;
6. recreate/restore ACMS database from pre-deploy dump;
7. switch to previous known-good app image/SHA;
8. start previous ACMS;
9. verify previous Alembic revision;
10. run complete smoke test;
11. verify LLDAP login path;
12. verify expected core data exists;
13. exit maintenance mode only after validation succeeds;
14. record rollback in release history.

The rollback script must refuse database restore unless:

- a valid pre-deploy backup exists;
- checksum validation succeeds;
- the backup belongs to the current deployment transaction;
- the deployment has not already been accepted and reopened for normal writes.

If any guard fails, stop and request human direction.

### Level 3 — VM/PBS disaster recovery

Use when:

- PostgreSQL volume is damaged;
- Docker/host state is corrupted;
- automated DB restore fails;
- filesystem damage occurred;
- VM cannot boot;
- previous release cannot be recovered locally.

Recovery:

1. isolate/offline the failed VM;
2. restore latest known-good ACMS VM from Proxmox Backup Server;
3. boot restored VM;
4. verify Git SHA / ACMS version;
5. verify PostgreSQL;
6. run smoke tests;
7. verify LLDAP login;
8. confirm agent/work data.

Do not destroy the failed VM until recovery is verified.

## 9. Dedicated rollback command

Implement:

`deploy/rollback.sh`

Default:

```bash
deploy/rollback.sh
```

returns to immediately previous known-good release.

Optional:

```bash
deploy/rollback.sh <release-id>
```

The script must:

- show current release;
- show target rollback release;
- verify required backup/image;
- refuse unknown/untrusted release;
- stop writes;
- restore app;
- restore DB if required;
- run health/smoke validation;
- print final Git SHA and DB revision;
- write rollback log/handoff.

## 10. Automatic rollback triggers

Automatically rollback before release acceptance if any of these occur:

- `/health` fails;
- database connectivity fails;
- Alembic migration fails;
- app container repeatedly crashes;
- HTTPS proxy cannot reach ACMS;
- access controls fail;
- expected API route returns fatal errors;
- UI login page fails;
- required work/agent pages fail;
- migration revision differs from expected;
- smoke tests fail;
- app startup times out.

If automated tests pass but the human reports a severe regression immediately after deployment, the human may instruct:

`rollback ACMS`

and the agent should rollback instead of attempting speculative live repair first.

## 11. Post-deploy validation

Before marking a release successful, perform repeated health checks, not one request.

At minimum:

- multiple `/health` checks;
- database connectivity;
- container restart count;
- logs scanned for repeated exceptions;
- UI login page reachable;
- new feature exercised;
- old critical feature exercised.

For each new feature, add feature-specific smoke checks.

## 12. Immediate synchronization step

Before new feature development, compare production:

```bash
git -C /opt/acms rev-parse HEAD
```

with repository:

```bash
git rev-parse origin/main
```

Do not infer deployed code solely from `ACMS v0.3.0`.

Current `main` already includes work-orchestration backend functionality.

If production is behind:

1. merge the safe-release/rollback PR first;
2. obtain human authorization to deploy;
3. deploy it;
4. then deploy the current approved `main`;
5. verify migrations/work APIs;
6. document exact live SHA.

## 15. Feature Slice 3 — Heartbeat + live status

**Branch:** `feat/ACMS-033-agent-heartbeat-status`

Implement heartbeat/liveness.

Show:

- last contact;

Suggested states:

```text
HEALTHY
IDLE
WORKING
STALE
UNREACHABLE

```

Do not derive detailed progress solely from heartbeat.

Use configurable stale thresholds and active reconciliation after missed contact rather than constant polling.

Capability-aware actions:

- send work;

- pause;

Hide/disable unsupported controls.

**Branch:** `feat/ACMS-015-peer-review`

## 22. Keep the live UI evolving

Do not wait for a separate late "dashboard phase."

If backend capability is useful to a human operator, expose it in the same PR or the immediately following small PR.

The temporary Jinja2 UI may remain while workflows evolve rapidly.

Consider React/Next.js only after workflows stabilize enough to justify the extra frontend surface.

## 23. Update `SPRINT.md`

Replace the stale foundation sprint with:

> Make the live ACMS useful for human work management while establishing a deterministic, AI-agent-executable rollback mechanism.

First sprint scope:

1. safe release/rollback tooling;
2. Work Management UI;
3. Agent Detail page;
4. live deployment after human approval.

Do not pull A2A control into the same sprint unless those complete cleanly.

## 24. Database migration rules

Every schema-changing PR must state whether its migration is backward-compatible with the previous app.

Prefer expand/contract:

### Release N
Add new structures while old behavior still works.

### Release N+1/N+2
Remove old structures only after no live code depends on them.

Avoid same-release destructive renames/drops and irreversible data transformations.

Destructive migration requires explicit human approval and restore testing.

## 25. Feature flags

Add a simple environment/config-backed feature-flag mechanism before risky agent-control features.

Example:

```text
ACMS_FEATURE_WORK_UI=true
ACMS_FEATURE_A2A_CONTROL=false
ACMS_FEATURE_HEARTBEAT=false
```

Feature flags are not a substitute for rollback. They are a quick disable mechanism.

## 26. Agent Bridge compatibility

When A2A features ship:

- preserve protocol version fields;
- use capability negotiation;
- do not assume all bridges upgrade together;
- keep compatibility with current and immediately previous profile version when practical;
- clearly flag incompatible bridges in the UI.

Rollback must not require simultaneous downgrade of every agent container.

## 27. Test layers

Each feature PR should include applicable:

- unit tests;
- PostgreSQL integration tests;
- API tests;
- UI tests;
- deployment smoke tests;
- migration rollback validation when schema changes.

For schema changes, validate:

```text
old DB
  ↓ backup
new migration
  ↓ new app test
rollback/restore
  ↓
old app runs again
```

## 28. Deployment decision tree

```text
Human authorized deployment?
 ├─ NO → stop
 └─ YES
      ↓
Preflight healthy?
 ├─ NO → stop, no changes
 └─ YES
      ↓
Backup verified?
 ├─ NO → stop, no migration
 └─ YES
      ↓
Deploy target
      ↓
Migration succeeds?
 ├─ NO → rollback DB + app
 └─ YES
      ↓
App healthy?
 ├─ NO → rollback
 └─ YES
      ↓
Smoke tests pass?
 ├─ NO → rollback
 └─ YES
      ↓
Feature-specific test passes?
 ├─ NO → rollback
 └─ YES
      ↓
Mark release known-good
```

## 29. Rollback prohibitions

The rollback agent must never:

- guess the previous release;
- blindly run `alembic downgrade`;
- delete the only verified backup;
- restore a dump with failed checksum;
- serve writes during database restore;
- rewrite Git history;
- patch production code directly;
- deploy an unreviewed branch as a permanent fix;
- hide a failed rollback;
- destroy the failed VM before recovery is verified.

A corrective PR fixes `main` later.

## 30. Recovery layers

Use three separate layers:

### Git
Source history and previous application versions.

### Pre-deploy PostgreSQL dump
Fast deterministic app/database rollback.

### Proxmox Backup Server
Whole-VM disaster recovery.

Do not treat any one layer as a substitute for the others.

# AGENTS.md

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous agents working in ACMS.

## Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope, approve significant decisions, define mandatory governance boundaries, and approve merges.

Agents may inspect the repository, plan, implement approved requirements, decompose approved scope into tasks, create tests, update affected documentation, create handoffs, add out-of-scope discoveries to `FUTURE_WORK.md`, draft ADRs/TDRs, and execute non-destructive work inside explicitly approved scope/budget/security/environment boundaries.

Agents must not silently add/remove product scope, promote future work, accept their own new significant ADR, merge their own feature work, exceed a budget, cross tenant/context boundaries, deploy to production, alter credentials/permissions, perform destructive infrastructure actions, or modify protected Organization/Product/Project/Feature context without required human authorization.

## Read before planning

For every meaningful change read:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `SPRINT.md`
4. `docs/BUSINESS_PROBLEMS.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. relevant accepted/proposed ADRs
7. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope/follow-up work is involved
8. `INITIAL_IDEAS.md` when source provenance matters

## Requirement discipline

Implementation must trace to one or more `ACMS-REQ-###` entries. Before editing summarize the requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, files/components likely to change, assumptions, and validation.

If work is outside approved requirements, ask for human approval or record it in `FUTURE_WORK.md`.

## Future-work provenance

Every future-work item must identify one of:

- **Human-Directed**
- **Agent-Discovered**
- **Source-Derived**

Human-Directed does not mean approved; it remains future scope until promoted.

## Centralized governance, decentralized execution

ACMS controls approved assignment, scope, budget, context access, permissions, and governance. It must not become the synchronous permission service for every shell command/tool call/test/retry.

A worker agent may autonomously execute implementation details inside its approved envelope.

An agent may have:

- one primary ACMS work assignment; and
- zero or more registered background routines/cron jobs.

## Context authority

Protected context levels:

- Organization
- Product
- Project
- Feature

Agents may propose changes but may not modify these without human authorization or explicit delegated authority.

Agent, Task, and Live Session context may be modified within approved policy.

If approved scope appears wrong, execute it as far as reasonably possible, preserve state, create a handoff, and propose the alternate direction instead of silently changing scope.

## Handoffs

Meaningful work must produce/update a Markdown handoff recording:

1. assigned goal and requirement IDs;
2. what was attempted;
3. what actually changed;
4. what the agent believes the current state is;
5. validation and results;
6. blockers/assumptions/unresolved questions;
7. recommended next action.

## ADR policy

Human-explicit durable architecture decisions may be recorded as `Accepted`. Agent-originated significant decisions remain `Proposed` until human approval.

## Approval timeout policy

- Reversible in-scope decision: checkpoint/preserve state, use documented fallback, continue if safe.
- Consequential but deferable action: defer that action and continue unrelated work.
- Mandatory human gate: never infer approval from timeout; hold/deny the gated action and continue only unrelated authorized work.

## Security

- Never commit credentials/secrets/customer-sensitive data.
- External/customer agents are a separate trust domain.
- Internal Agent Bridge endpoints are private-network/VPN endpoints unless an accepted ADR says otherwise.
- Do not weaken authentication/authorization/audit to simplify implementation.

## Git workflow

The documentation bootstrap is human-authorized for direct import. After bootstrap, feature work must use a dedicated branch and PR.

Recommended branch: `type/ACMS-short-description`.

Recommended commit: `type(scope): short description (ACMS-REQ-###)` plus issue/ticket when available.

Every feature PR must list requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, validation, risks, ADR/TDR changes, future work, and reviewer focus. Agents do not self-merge.

curl -fsS --max-time 8 http://127.0.0.1:8000/api/v1/agents'\n\necho \"==> HTTPS UI (via reverse proxy: $BASE_URL)\"\ncheck \"HTTPS reachable (self-signed ok)\" curl -kfsS --max-time 8 \"$BASE_URL/ui/login\"\nBASE_HOST=\"$(echo \"$BASE_URL\" | sed -E 's|^https?://||')\"\ncheck \"HTTP redirects to HTTPS\" bash -c \"curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' --max-time 8 \\\"http://$BASE_HOST/ui/login\\\" | grep -q 301\"\ncheck \"login page served over TLS\" bash -c \"curl -kfsS --max-time 8 '$BASE_URL/ui/login' | grep -qi 'sign in'\"\ncheck \"UI redirects unauthenticated users\" bash -c \"curl -k -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' --max-time 8 '$BASE_URL/ui/' | grep -q 303\"\ncheck \"registry API not exposed via proxy\" bash -c '! ### [2026-09-26T23:28:37.747880+00:00] assistant

## Report — Drill 3: `Release ACCEPTED` ✅ The pipeline is proven

With #8 merged, I ran the full sequence on CT122:

**1. Bootstrap hop → `363cf3c`** (manual, since the on-box tooling was still the drill-2-buggy copy). One hiccup self-inflicted and recovered mid-hop: compose v2 container names broke my `docker exec` name — lesson logged (use `compose exec`, it's service-name agnostic).

**2. Drill 3 — first clean end-to-end `release.sh` run.** Same tip, no code change, auto-rollback armed but **never needed**:

| Phase | Result |
|---|---|
| Preflight + verified backup (PGDMP, sha256) | ✅ |
| Maintenance window (ON → 503 confirmed → migrate → OFF) | ✅ |
| App ready | **2 s** |
| Stage-1 validation (app, under maintenance) | 6/6 |
| Stage-2 validation (full, via proxy) | 12/12 |
| **Result** | **`Release ACCEPTED`, exit 0** |

Both drill-2 fixes held under live execution — image tag clean, no health false-fail. Ledger is correct (`current`/`previous`/history.jsonl), no open transaction, 4 verified backups on disk.

**3. Post-drill sweep found one more latent tooling bug → [PR #9](https://github.com/startupteams/acms-project-framework/pull/9)** (not a release gate — `validate-release.sh` was correct): the standalone operator `smoke-test.sh` curls `127.0.0.1:8000` **from the VM host**, but the app publishes no host ports by design (nginx is the only ingress) — those checks could never pass. It also counted failures but **always exited 0**, so it couldn't gate anything. PR #9 fixes both, adds the Work UI check, and exits properly.

**Live production right now:** `363cf3c` / v0.4.0 / rev `0002` — Work UI, Agents, System all serving; every check green.

**On your desk:** merge PR #9 → I'll do the tooling-only hop + run the fixed smoke on-box → then start **Feature Slice 2** (Agent Detail + routines page, plan §14). After that, the only remaining §31 milestone is the live rollback drill, which the Level-2 chain has already effectively proven twice.

A2A delivery is slice 4.\n34|DELIVERY_STATE = \"Recorded assignment only — delivery to the agent (A2A) is not implemented yet\"\n35|\n36|_STATUSES = [s.value for s in WorkItemStatus]\n37|_KINDS = [k.value for k in WorkItemKind]\n38|_CLOSE_STATUSES = (\"COMPLETED\", \"RELEASED\", \"SUSPENDED\")\n39|\n40|\n41|def _require_admin(user) -> None:\n42|    \"\"\"Mutations are Administrator-only in record.status,\n64|        \"scope_markdown\": record.scope_markdown,\n65|        \"created_at\": record.created_at,\n66|        \"updated_at\": record.updated_at,\n67|    }\n68|\n69|\n70|async def items}\n73|\n74|\n75|@router.get(\"\")\n76|async def work_list(\n77|    request: Request,\n78|    status_filter: str | None = None,\n79|    kind_filter: str | None = None,\n80| items}\n85|    if status_filter in _STATUSES:\n86|        items = [i for i in items if i.status == status_filter]\n87|    if kind_filter in _KINDS:\n88|        items = [i for i in items if i.kind == kind_filter]\n89|    # Newest first for operator ergonomics.\n90|    items = sorted(items, key=lambda i: i.updated_at, reverse=True)\n91|    context = {\n92|        \"user\": user,\n93|        \"items\": [_view_item(i, parents.get(i.parent_id)) for i in items],\n94|        \"statuses\": _STATUSES,\n95|        \"kinds\": _KINDS,\n96|        \"status_filter\": status_filter or \"\",\n97| {"success": true, "name": "hermes-agent", "description": "Configure, extend, or contribute to Hermes Agent.", "tags": ["hermes", "setup", "configuration", "multi-agent", "spawning", "cli", "gateway", "development"], "related_skills": ["claude-code", "codex", "opencode"], "content": "---\nname: hermes-agent\ndescription: \"Configure, extend, or contribute to Hermes Agent.\"\nversion: 2.0.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent + Teknium\nlicense: MIT\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [hermes, setup, configuration, multi-agent, spawning, cli, gateway, development]\n    homepage: https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent\n    related_skills: [claude-code, codex, opencode]\n---\n\n# Hermes Agent\n\nHermes Agent is an open-source AI agent framework by Nous Research that runs in your terminal, messaging platforms, and IDEs. It belongs to the same category as Claude Code (Anthropic), Codex (OpenAI), and OpenClaw — autonomous coding and task-execution agents that use tool calling to interact with your system. Hermes works with any LLM provider (OpenRouter, Anthropic, OpenAI, DeepSeek, local models, and 15+ others) and runs on Linux, macOS, and WSL.\n\nWhat makes Hermes different:\n\n- **Self-improving through skills** — Hermes learns from experience by saving reusable procedures as skills. When it solves a complex problem, discovers a workflow, or gets corrected, it can persist that knowledge as a skill document that loads into future sessions. Skills accumulate over time, making the agent better at your specific tasks and environment.\n- **Persistent memory across sessions** — remembers who you are, your preferences, environment details, and lessons learned. Pluggable memory backends (built-in, Honcho, Mem0, and more) let you choose how memory works.\n- **Multi-platform gateway** — the same agent runs on Telegram, Discord, Slack, WhatsApp, Signal, Matrix, Email, and 10+ other platforms with full tool access, not just chat.\n- **Provider-agnostic** — swap models and providers mid-workflow without changing anything else. Credential pools rotate across multiple API keys automatically.\n- **Profiles** — run multiple independent Hermes instances with isolated configs, sessions, skills, and memory.\n- **Extensible** — plugins, MCP servers, custom tools, webhook triggers, cron scheduling, and the full Python ecosystem.\n\nPeople use Hermes for software development, research, system administration, data analysis, content creation, home automation, and anything else that benefits from an AI agent with persistent context and full system access.\n\n**This skill helps you work with Hermes Agent effectively** — setting it up, configuring features, spawning additional agent instances, troubleshooting issues, finding the right commands and settings, and understanding how the system works when you need to extend or contribute to it.\n\n**Docs:** https://hermes-agent.nousresearch.com/docs/\n\n## WHEN TO USE\n\n- **Setting up or reconfiguring Hermes** — model, provider, gateway, tools, permissions\n- **Troubleshooting** — auth failures, tool errors, gateway connection issues, crashes\n- **Extending Hermes** — adding custom tools, plugins, skills, MCP servers, cron jobs\n- **Managing profiles** — creating, cloning, syncing, exporting/importing profiles\n- **Understanding Hermes internals** — how sessions, memory, skills, and toolsets work\n- **Contributing to Hermes** — reporting bugs, submitting PRs, running tests\n- **Configuration queries** — \"How do I set up X?\", \"Where is the config?\", \"Why is Y happening?\"\n\n## QUICK REFERENCE\n\n```bash\n# Essential commands\nhermes              # Interactive chat\nhermes setup        # Setup wizard\nhermes model        # Change model/provider\nhermes config edit  # Edit config.yaml\nhermes doctor       # Health check\nhermes tools        # Enable/disable toolsets\nhermes skills list  # List installed skills\nhermes profile list # List profiles\nhermes cron list    # List cron jobs\nhermes gateway run  # Start gateway\n```\n\n## Architecture Overview\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                    Hermes Agent                             │\n├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n│                                                             │\n│  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐   │\n│  │ CLI      │  │ Gateway  │  │ Cron     │  │ ACP      │   │\n│  │ (hermes) │  │ (Telegram│  │ (cron)   │  │ (IDE)    │   │\n│  │          │  │  etc.)   │  │          │  │          │   │\n│  └────┬─────┘  └────┬─────┘  └────┬─────┘  └────┬─────┘   │\n│       │              │              │              │         │\n│       └──────────────┼──────────────┼──────────────┘         │\n│                      ▼              ▼                       │\n│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐       │\n│  │              Agent Core                         │       │\n│  │  • Skill Loader  • Memory Manager               │       │\n│  │  • Tool Dispatcher • Session Manager            │       │\n│  │  • Context Compressor • Credential Pool          │       │\n│  └────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘       │\n│                       ▼                                    │\n│  ┌──────────┐ ┌──────────┐ ┌──────────┐ ┌──────────┐      │\n│  │ terminal │ │ file     │ │ web      │ │ browser  │      │\n│  │          │ │ search/  │ │ search/  │ │ automation│      │\n│  │          │ │ write    │ │ fetch    │ │          │      │\n│  └──────────┘ └──────────┘ └──────────┘ └──────────┘      │\n│                                                             │\n├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n│  Profiles: ~/.hermes/profiles/<name>/                       │\n│  Config: ~/.hermes/config.yaml                              │\n│  Skills: ~/.hermes/skills/ + ~/.hermes/profiles/<name>/...  │\n│  Sessions: ~/.hermes/sessions/                              │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n## INTEGRATION POINTS\n\n| Related Skill | How They Connect |\n|---------------|-----------------|\n| **claude-code** | Claude Code runs as an external agent via Hermes terminal. Use `terminal()` to invoke, `pty=true` for interactive mode, `background=true` for long tasks. |\n| **codex** | OpenAI Codex runs the same way — `terminal()` with either print mode (`-p`) or PTY mode. Cron jobs can trigger codex workflows. |\n| **opencode** | Provider-agnostic coding agent. Same invocation pattern via `terminal()`. Useful for multi-provider comparisons. |\n| **ruflo** | Ruflo is the orchestrator layer above Hermes. Hermes provides the terminal/execution environment; ruflo coordinates multi-agent swarms. |\n| **openharness** | OpenHarness provides the ohmo persona system. SOUL.md can be injected into Hermes via `--append-system-prompt-file`. |\n| **nomos** | NOMOS workflows can invoke Hermes via terminal. Use Hermes as the execution engine within NOMOS state machines. |\n| **armory** | Armory skills can be installed via `hermes skills install`. Install armory skill packages into `~/.hermes/skills/` or profile skill dirs. |\n| **caveman** | Token compression for Hermes output. Add to CLAUDE.md or agent system prompt for compressed responses. |\n| **hermes-agent** | This skill — the authoritative reference for configuring and extending Hermes itself. |\n\n## PITFALLS & GOTCHAS\n\n1. **Config changes require a fresh session** — Most config settings (`security.redact_secrets`, `approvals.mode`, toolsets) are snapshotted at import time. To apply changes, run `hermes config set KEY VAL` then start a new session (`/reset` in chat or `hermes` again in CLI). This is deliberate — it prevents an LLM from flipping the toggle on itself mid-task.\n\n2. **Secret redaction is OFF by default** — Tool output passes through unmodified. If you need API keys and secrets masked, run `hermes config set security.redact_secrets true` and restart. This does NOT affect `approvals.mode` — they are independent settings.\n\n3. **YOLO mode is NOT safe for secrets** — Setting `approvals.mode: off` or `--yolo` disables command approval but does NOT disable secret redaction. These are independent controls.\n\n4. **Toolset changes don't apply mid-conversation** — Enabling/disabling toolsets takes effect on `/reset` (new session) only. They do NOT apply mid-conversation to preserve prompt caching. Tell users this explicitly.\n\n5. **Cron jobs run in a fresh session** — Cron cron-run sessions have no current-chat context. Prompts must be fully self-contained. Skills and context_from are the only way to inject prior state.\n\n6. **Script-based cron jobs expect scripts in the profile's scripts/ directory** — Not `~/.hermes/scripts/`. The scheduler looks for scripts at `~/.hermes/profiles/<profile>/scripts/<name>.sh`.\n\n7. **tmux sessions persist after Claude Code exits** — If Claude Code crashes or is killed mid-session, the tmux session remains. Always clean up with `tmux kill-session -t <name>` when done.\n\n8. **Gateway auth requires browser** — OAuth verification codes pasted via tmux `send-keys` fail because tmux treats them as password input. The user must log in on their local machine with a browser for initial auth.\n\n9. **Print mode can't write files** — Claude Code in `-p` mode refuses file writes. Use shell redirect (`> output.md 2>&1`) or the `write_file` tool instead.\n\n10. **Fable 5 session token budget** — Fable 5 has a per-session token budget. Running multiple parallel Fable 5 jobs can exhaust the budget mid-run. Run them sequentially or with max 2 concurrent.\n\n11. **Session resumption requires same directory** — `--continue` only works in the same working directory. For cross-directory work, capture the session ID and resume explicitly.\n\n12. **Context degradation is real** — AI output quality measurably degrades above 70% context window usage. Monitor with `/context` and proactively `/compact`.\n\n13. **`/exit` opens agent selector in OpenCode** — It's NOT a valid exit command. Use Ctrl+C to exit OpenCode sessions.\n\n14. **OpenCode needs `pty=true` for interactive mode** — OpenCode is a TUI app. Without PTY, it hangs. `opencode run` (bounded tasks) does NOT need PTY.\n\n15. **OAuth tokens are stored in `~/.hermes/auth.json`** — If auth is failing, check this file. Credential pools rotate automatically but can get stuck — use `hermes auth reset PROVIDER` to clear exhaustion.\n\n## Quick Start\n\n```bash\n# Install\ncurl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/main/scripts/install.sh | bash\n\n# Interactive chat (default)\nhermes\n\n# Single query\nhermes chat -q \"What is the capital of France?\"\n\n# Setup wizard\nhermes setup\n\n# Change model/provider\nhermes model\n\n# Check health\nhermes doctor\n```\n\n---\n\n## CLI Reference\n\n### Global Flags\n\n```\nhermes [flags] [command]\n\n  --version, -V             Show version\n  --resume, -r SESSION      Resume session by ID or title\n  --continue, -c [NAME]     Resume by name, or most recent session\n  --worktree, -w            Isolated git worktree mode (parallel agents)\n  --skills, -s SKILL        Preload skills (comma-separate or repeat)\n  --profile, -p NAME        Use a named profile\n  --yolo                    Skip dangerous command approval\n  --pass-session-id         Include session ID in system prompt\n```\n\nNo subcommand defaults to `chat`.\n\n### Chat\n\n```\nhermes chat [flags]\n  -q, --query TEXT          Single query, non-interactive\n  -m, --model MODEL         Model (e.g. anthropic/claude-sonnet-4)\n  -t, --toolsets LIST       Comma-separated toolsets\n  --provider PROVIDER       Force provider (openrouter, anthropic, nous, etc.)\n  -v, --verbose             Verbose output\n  -Q, --quiet               Suppress banner, spinner, tool previews\n  --checkpoints             Enable filesystem checkpoints (/rollback)\n  --source TAG              Session source tag (default: cli)\n```\n\n### Configuration\n\n```\nhermes setup [section]      Interactive wizard (model|terminal|gateway|tools|agent)\nhermes model                Interactive model/provider picker\nhermes config               View current config\nhermes config edit          Open config.yaml in $EDITOR\nhermes config set KEY VAL   Set a config value\nhermes config path          Print config.yaml path\nhermes config env-path      Print .env path\nhermes config check         Check for missing/outdated config\nhermes config migrate       Update config with new options\nhermes login [--provider P] OAuth login (nous, openai-codex)\nhermes logout               Clear stored auth\nhermes doctor [--fix]       Check dependencies and config\nhermes status [--all]       Show component status\n```\n\n### Tools & Skills\n\n```\nhermes tools                Interactive tool enable/disable (curses UI)\nhermes tools list           Show all tools and status\nhermes tools enable NAME    Enable a toolset\nhermes tools disable NAME   Disable a toolset\n\nhermes skills list          List installed skills\nhermes skills search QUERY  Search the skills hub\nhermes skills install ID    Install a skill (ID can be a hub identifier OR a direct https://…/SKILL.md URL; pass --name to override when frontmatter has no name)\nhermes skills inspect ID    Preview without installing\nhermes skills config        Enable/disable skills per platform\nhermes skills check         Check for updates\nhermes skills update        Update outdated skills\nhermes skills uninstall N   Remove a hub skill\nhermes skills publish PATH  Publish to registry\nhermes skills browse        Browse all available skills\nhermes skills tap add REPO  Add a GitHub repo as skill source\n```\n\n### MCP Servers\n\n```\nhermes mcp serve            Run Hermes as an MCP server\nhermes mcp add NAME         Add an MCP server (--url or --command)\nhermes mcp remove NAME      Remove an MCP server\nhermes mcp list             List configured servers\nhermes mcp test NAME        Test connection\nhermes mcp configure NAME   Toggle tool selection\n```\n\n### Gateway (Messaging Platforms)\n\n```\nhermes gateway run          Start gateway foreground\nhermes gateway install      Install as background service\nhermes gateway start/stop   Control the service\nhermes gateway restart      Restart the service\nhermes gateway status       Check status\nhermes gateway setup        Configure platforms\n```\n\nSupported platforms: Telegram, Discord, Slack, WhatsApp, Signal, Email, SMS, Matrix, Mattermost, Home Assistant, DingTalk, Feishu, WeCom, BlueBubbles (iMessage), Weixin (WeChat), API Server, Webhooks. Open WebUI connects via the API Server adapter.\n\nPlatform docs: https://hermes-agent.nousresearch.com/docs/user-guide/messaging/\n\n### Sessions\n\n```\nhermes sessions list        List recent sessions\nhermes sessions browse      Interactive picker\nhermes sessions export OUT  Export to JSONL\nhermes sessions rename ID T Rename a session\nhermes sessions delete ID   Delete a session\nhermes sessions prune       Clean up old sessions (--older-than N days)\nhermes sessions stats       Session store statistics\n```\n\n### Cron Jobs\n\n```\nhermes cron list            List jobs (--all for disabled)\nhermes cron create SCHED    Create: '30m', 'every 2h', '0 9 * * *'\nhermes cron edit ID         Edit schedule, prompt, delivery\nhermes cron pause/resume ID Control job state\nhermes cron run ID          Trigger on next tick\nhermes cron remove ID       Delete a job\nhermes cron status          Scheduler status\n```\n\nFor script-only cron jobs, prefer `no_agent=true` and put the executable script or wrapper in the active profile's `scripts/` directory; the cron tool expects a relative script filename, not an absolute path. For backing up a live Hermes profile/home Git repository such as `~/.hermes`, verify the live repository path rather than creating a separate clone. See `references/profile-repo-hourly-git-sync.md`.\n\n### Webhooks\n\n```\nhermes webhook subscribe N  Create route at /webhooks/<name>\nhermes webhook list         List subscriptions\nhermes webhook remove NAME  Remove a subscription\nhermes webhook test NAME    Send a test POST\n```\n\n### Profiles\n\n```\nhermes profile list         List all profiles\nhermes profile create NAME  Create (--clone, --clone-all, --clone-from)\nhermes profile use NAME     Set sticky default\nhermes profile delete NAME  Delete a profile\nhermes profile show NAME    Show details\nhermes profile alias NAME   Manage wrapper scripts\nhermes profile rename A B   Rename a profile\nhermes profile export NAME  Export to tar.gz\nhermes profile import FILE  Import from archive\n```\n\nWhen syncing a profile `SOUL.md` from a GitHub agent identity file or installing companion skills from direct URLs, preserve exact source bytes and use non-interactive install flags (`--category`, `--yes`). See `references/profile-soul-sync-and-external-skill-install.md`.\n\n### Credential Pools\n\n```\nhermes auth add             Interactive credential wizard\nhermes auth list [PROVIDER] List pooled credentials\nhermes auth remove P INDEX  Remove by provider + index\nhermes auth reset PROVIDER  Clear exhaustion status\n```\n\n### Other\n\n```\nhermes insights [--days N]  Usage analytics\nhermes update               Update to latest version\nhermes pairing list/approve/revoke  DM authorization\nhermes plugins list/install/remove  Plugin management\nhermes honcho setup/status  Honcho memory integration (requires honcho plugin)\nhermes memory setup/status/off  Memory provider config\nhermes completion bash|zsh  Shell completions\nhermes acp                  ACP server (IDE integration)\nhermes claw migrate         Migrate from OpenClaw\nhermes uninstall            Uninstall Hermes\n```\n\n---\n\n## Slash Commands (In-Session)\n\nType these during an interactive chat session.\n\n### Session Control\n```\n/new (/reset)        Fresh session\n/clear               Clear screen + new session (CLI)\n/retry               Resend last message\n/undo                Remove last exchange\n/title [name]        Name the session\n/compress            Manually compress context\n/stop                Kill background processes\n/rollback [N]        Restore filesystem checkpoint\n/background <prompt> Run prompt in background\n/queue <prompt>      Queue for next turn\n/resume [name]       Resume a named session\n```\n\n### Configuration\n```\n/config [Subdirectory context discovered: .hermes/hermes-agent/AGENTS.md]
# Hermes Agent - Development Guide

Instructions for AI coding assistants and developers working on the hermes-agent codebase.

**Never give up on the right solution.**

## What Hermes Is

Hermes is a personal AI agent that runs the same agent core across a CLI, a
messaging gateway (Telegram, Discord, Slack, and ~20 other platforms), a TUI,
and an Electron desktop app. It learns across sessions (memory + skills),
delegates to subagents, runs scheduled jobs, and drives a real terminal and
browser. It is extended primarily through **plugins and skills**, not by
growing the core.

Two properties shape almost every design decision and are the lens for
reviewing any change:

- **Per-conversation prompt caching is sacred.** A long-lived conversation
  reuses a cached prefix every turn. Anything that mutates past context,
  swaps toolsets, or rebuilds the system prompt mid-conversation invalidates
  that cache and multiplies the user's cost. We do not do it (the one
  exception is context compression).
- **The core is a narrow waist; capability lives at the edges.** Every model
  tool we add is sent on every API call, so the bar for a new *core* tool is
  high. Most new capability should arrive as a CLI command + skill, a
  service-gated tool, or a plugin — not as core surface.

## Contribution Rubric — What We Want / What We Don't

This is the project's intent layer. Use it two ways:

1. **For humans and for your own work** — what gets merged and what gets
   rejected, so a contribution aims at the target.
2. **For automated review (the triage sweeper)** — guidance on when a PR is
   safe to close on the three allowed reasons (`implemented_on_main`,
   `cannot_reproduce`, `incoherent`) and, just as important, **when NOT to
   close** one. Taste-based "we don't want this / out of scope" closes are NOT
   an automated decision — those stay with a human maintainer. The sweeper's
   job here is to recognize design intent and *avoid wrongly closing a
   legitimate contribution*, not to make the won't-implement call itself.

Read the balance right: Hermes ships a **lot** — most merges are bug fixes to
real reported behavior, and the product surface (platforms, channels,
providers, models, desktop/TUI features) expands aggressively and on purpose.
The restraint below is aimed squarely at the **core agent + the model tool
schema**, the one place where every addition is paid for on every API call.
"Smallest footprint" governs *how a capability is wired into the core*, NOT
whether the product is allowed to grow. We are expansive at the edges and
conservative at the waist.

### What we want

- **Fix real bugs, well.** The bulk of what lands is `fix(...)` against an
  actual reported symptom. A good fix reproduces the symptom on current
  `main`, points to the exact line where it manifests, and fixes the whole bug
  class — sibling call paths included — not just the one site the reporter hit.
- **Expand reach at the edges.** New platform adapters, channels, providers,
  models, and desktop/TUI/dashboard features are welcome and land routinely,
  including large ones (a new messaging channel, a session-cap feature, a
  Windows PTY bridge). Breadth in the product is a goal, not a footprint
  concern — as long as it integrates with the existing setup/config UX
  (`hermes tools`, `hermes setup`, auto-install) rather than bolting on a raw
  env var.
- **Refactor god-files into clean modules.** Extracting a multi-thousand-line
  cluster out of `cli.py` / `run_agent.py` / `gateway/run.py` into a focused
  mixin or module is wanted work, even when the diff is huge and mechanical
  (large `+N/-N` refactors merge regularly). The "every line traces to the
  request" test applies to *feature* PRs; a declared refactor's request IS the
  extraction.
- **Keep the core narrow.** New *model tools* are the expensive exception —
  every tool ships on every API call. Prefer, in order: extend existing code →
  CLI command + skill → service-gated tool (`check_fn`) → plugin → MCP server
  in the catalog → new core tool (last resort). See "The Footprint Ladder."
- **Extend, don't duplicate.** Before adding a module/manager/hook, check
  whether existing infrastructure already covers the use case. When several PRs
  integrate the same *category*, design one shared interface instead of merging
  them one at a time (see the ABC + orchestrator note under the Footprint
  Ladder).
- **Behavior contracts over snapshots.** Tests should assert how two pieces of
  data must relate (invariants), not freeze a current value (model lists,
  config version literals, enumeration counts). See "Don't write
  change-detector tests."
- **E2E validation, not just green unit mocks.** For anything touching
  resolution chains, config propagation, security boundaries, remote
  backends, or file/network I/O, exercise the real path with real imports
  against a temp `HERMES_HOME`. Mocks hide integration bugs.
- **Cache-, alternation-, and invariant-safe.** Preserve prompt caching, strict
  message role alternation (never two same-role messages in a row; never a
  synthetic user message injected mid-loop), and a system prompt that is
  byte-stable for the life of a conversation.
- **Contributor credit preserved.** Salvage external work by cherry-picking
  (rebase-merge) so authorship survives in git history; don't reimplement from
  scratch when you can build on top.

### What we don't want (rejected even when well-built)

- **Speculative infrastructure.** Hooks, callbacks, or extension points with no
  concrete consumer. Adding a hook is easy; removing one after plugins depend
  on it is hard. A hook is NOT speculative if a contributor has a real, stated
  use case — even if the consumer ships separately.
- **New `HERMES_*` env vars for non-secret config.** `.env` is for secrets
  only (API keys, tokens, passwords). All behavioral settings — timeouts,
  thresholds, feature flags, display prefs — go in `config.yaml`. Bridge to an
  internal env var if the mechanism needs one, but user-facing docs point to
  `config.yaml`. Reject PRs that tell users to "set X in your .env" unless X
  is a credential.
- **A new core tool when terminal + file already do the job, or when a skill
  would.** If the only barrier is file visibility on a remote backend, fix the
  mount, not the toolset.
- **Lazy-reading escape hatches on instructional tools.** No `offset`/`limit`
  pagination on tools that load content the agent must read fully (skills,
  prompts, playbooks). Models will read page 1 and skip the rest.
- **"Fixes" that destroy the feature they secure.** A mitigation that kills the
  feature's purpose is the wrong mitigation. Read the original commit's intent
  (`git log -p -S`) before restricting behavior; find a fix that preserves the
  feature.
- **Outbound telemetry / usage attribution without opt-in gating.** No new
  analytics, third-party identifier tagging, or attribution tags until a
  generic user-facing opt-in (config gate + setup prompt + `hermes tools`
  toggle) exists. Park behind a label, do not merge.
- **Change-detector tests, cache-breaking mid-conversation, dead code wired in
  without E2E proof, and plugins that touch core files.** Plugins live in their
  own directory and work within the ABCs/hooks we provide; if a plugin needs
  more, widen the generic plugin surface, don't special-case it in core.
- **Third-party products / other people's projects integrated into the core
  tree.** Observability backends, vendor SaaS integrations, analytics dashboards,
  and similar "someone else's product" plugins do NOT land under `plugins/` in
  this repo. They place an ongoing maintenance burden on us to keep them working
  against a fast-moving core, for a backend we don't own. Ship them as a
  **standalone plugin repo** users install into `~/.hermes/plugins/` (or via a
  pip entry point), and promote them in the Nous Research Discord
  (`#plugins-skills-and-skins`). This is a coupling-and-maintenance decision, not

[Content of ACMS_COMBINED_SLICE_3_4_AGENT_HEARTBEAT_A2A_CONTROL_PLAN_2026_09.md]:
# ACMS Combined Slice 3 + 4 Execution Plan
## Agent Heartbeat, Session/Work Correlation, Context Telemetry, Reconciliation, A2A Control, and Hermes Agent Bridge

**Date:** 2026-09-26  
**Repository:** `startupteams/acms-project-framework`  
**Target:** MARION-IA-USA live ACMS  
**Execution:** AI-agent implementation on a feature branch, human-reviewed PR, then human-authorized deployment through the existing safe-release pipeline.

## 1. Goal

Combine the former heartbeat/live-status slice and A2A delivery/steering slice into one useful vertical feature.

At completion ACMS should be able to:

- distinguish bridge/connectivity health from whether the harness is actively running an agent turn;
- know the ACMS Work Item/Assignment that was approved;
- know the harness session ID/title and what the agent appears to think it is doing;
- correlate those two views through immutable ACMS identifiers;
- receive a full lightweight status heartbeat every 60 seconds;
- mark connectivity stale after 5 minutes without contact;
- actively reconcile after 1 continuous hour stale;
- reconcile every registered agent at least every 24 hours;
- monitor context-window use and warn before exhaustion;
- send work, steering, pause, resume, interrupt, and cancel when the harness has proven support for the required semantics;
- record important state/control changes as semantic events;
- request a Markdown handoff if the reported session persistently disagrees with the approved assignment;
- show all of this in the existing ACMS UI.

This is the point where ACMS begins acting as a real workforce control plane instead of only a management database.

---

## 2. ADR decisions

### 2.1 Accept ADR-0009 with amendments

Update `docs/adr/0009-safe-release-transaction.md` to **Accepted**.

Add:

1. the accumulated live drill/deployment evidence from the 2026-09-26 co-work handoff; and
2. explicit policy that:
   - production deployment means a human-authorized `deploy/release.sh` execution (or accepted successor);
   - after a deployment is authorized, rollback to the immediately previous known-good release is pre-authorized if release validation fails.

Do not change the already validated release/rollback mechanism merely to rewrite the ADR.

### 2.2 Add accepted heartbeat/reconciliation/control ADR

Create the next ADR number, expected to be `ADR-0010`.

Record these human-approved defaults:

```text
heartbeat interval              60 seconds
STALE threshold                 300 seconds / 5 minutes
stale reconciliation threshold 3600 seconds / 1 continuous hour stale
fleet-wide reconciliation       86400 seconds / 24 hours
```

Use configuration-backed values, e.g.:

```text
ACMS_HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_SECONDS=60
ACMS_HEARTBEAT_STALE_SECONDS=300
ACMS_STALE_RECONCILE_SECONDS=3600
ACMS_FLEET_RECONCILE_SECONDS=86400
```

These do not need ordinary UI controls in the MVP.

The ADR must also record:

- connectivity is distinct from `agent_running`;
- A2A owns A2A task-state enumerations;
- ACMS management scope/assignment is distinct from runtime state;
- reconciliation may automatically correct observed operational state;
- reconciliation must not silently change approved scope/assignment;
- operational disagreements are logged;
- heartbeat contains a complete lightweight status snapshot every 60 seconds;
- harness lifecycle capabilities are researched/tested before mapping;
- unsupported controls are explicitly unsupported, never faked;
- context-window telemetry is first-class operational data.

---

## 3. Requirements updates

Review `ACMS-REQ-031` through `ACMS-REQ-037` and clarify them where appropriate.

If the following approved concepts do not fit atomically, create new Priority-3 requirements using the next permanent IDs, expected to begin with `ACMS-REQ-052`.

### ACMS-REQ-052 - Human-readable work and assignment correlation identifiers

ACMS shall assign immutable human-readable identifiers to Work Items and primary assignments and communicate them to the executing bridge/harness.

Acceptance:

- Work Item has a unique immutable human-readable key plus its UUID.
- Assignment has a unique immutable human-readable key plus its UUID.
- keys are never reused;
- work key is communicated during work dispatch;
- assignment key is included in structured task/control metadata;
- session/title information can be compared with expected ACMS work;
- a later Jira/GitHub reference can coexist without replacing the ACMS key.

### ACMS-REQ-053 - Session/work alignment and drift detection

ACMS shall compare the agent's reported session/work identity to its approved primary assignment.

Acceptance:

- heartbeat contains session ID/title when available;
- ACMS knows expected work/assignment keys;
- ACMS derives `ALIGNED`, `UNKNOWN`, or `MISMATCH`;
- mismatch does not change approved scope;
- persistent mismatch can request a handoff;
- discrepancies are auditable.

### ACMS-REQ-054 - Context-window telemetry and warning

ACMS shall collect available current-context usage and display used tokens, maximum tokens, and utilization percentage.

Acceptance:

- current context usage is separate from lifetime/cumulative API usage;
- invalid values are not treated as valid counts;
- warning level is visible;
- explicit context-overflow/provider failures can be recorded as events.

---

## 4. Work and assignment identifiers

Keep UUIDs internally and add human-readable immutable keys.

Recommended:

```text
ACMS-WORK-000001
ACMS-ASG-000001
```

Allocate them collision-safely, preferably using PostgreSQL sequences or equivalent durable allocation.

Do not derive keys from mutable row counts.

### Session title convention

When ACMS dispatches primary work, request a harness title:

```text
[ACMS-WORK-000042] Implement Agent Bridge heartbeat
```

Also send structured metadata:

```text
acms_work_key: ACMS-WORK-000042
acms_assignment_key: ACMS-ASG-000017
```

A future external tracker reference is optional and separate:

```text
external_work_ref: optional
```

Do not make Jira a dependency.

---

## 5. Keep state dimensions independent

Do not create one giant ACMS state machine.

### 5.1 Connectivity, derived by ACMS

Use:

```text
UNKNOWN
HEALTHY
STALE
UNREACHABLE
```

Definitions:

- `UNKNOWN`: never successfully contacted.
- `HEALTHY`: heartbeat or successful reconciliation is recent.
- `STALE`: no successful contact for more than 5 minutes.
- `UNREACHABLE`: stale for at least 1 hour and active reconciliation failed.

Store `last_contact_source`, e.g. heartbeat/reconciliation/registration.

### 5.2 Agent execution, reported by harness

Use a tri-state field:

```text
agent_running = true
agent_running = false
agent_running = null/unknown
```

Do not invent ambiguous duplicate states such as READY/IDLE/STOPPED unless a harness exposes a separately defined fact.

For Hermes, `agent_running` means whether Hermes currently has an active agent execution, not whether the VM/gateway is reachable.

Therefore this is valid:

```text
Connectivity: HEALTHY
Agent Running: No
```

### 5.3 A2A task state

Use the A2A protocol's native task-state model.

Do not create parallel ACMS task-state names just to rename A2A states.

### 5.4 Management state

Keep Work Item and Assignment lifecycle independent.

Heartbeat/reconciliation must not silently change approved management scope.

---

## 6. Heartbeat payload

Every 60 seconds send a complete lightweight versioned status snapshot.

Suggested schema:

```yaml
schema_version: acms-heartbeat-v1

identity:
  acms_agent_id: ...
  bridge_id: ...
  bridge_version: ...
  harness: hermes
  harness_version: ...
  protocol_version: ...

observed_at: ...

assignment:
  acms_work_key: ACMS-WORK-000042
  acms_assignment_key: ACMS-ASG-000017

session:
  session_id: ...
  title: "[ACMS-WORK-000042] Implement Agent Bridge heartbeat"
  created_at: ...
  last_activity_at: ...
  agent_running: true

model:
  model_id: ...
  provider: ...

context:
  used_tokens: ...
  max_tokens: ...
  utilization_percent: ...

usage:
  session_total_tokens: ...
  cumulative_api_tokens: ...

platforms:
  connected:
    - telegram
```

### Important telemetry distinction

Do not conflate:

- current context-window tokens;
- session/lifetime tokens;
- cumulative API tokens;
- provider prompt-cache tokens;
- GPU/KV-cache utilization.

For this slice prioritize:

```text
context.used_tokens
context.max_tokens
context.utilization_percent
```

If other statistics are available, give them separately named fields.

The maximum context value may come from the harness/model route, LLM Manager model metadata, or a configured fallback. Record the source when practical. Do not fabricate a maximum.

---

## 7. Context warning behavior

Add configurable defaults:

```text
NORMAL    < 70%
ELEVATED  >= 70%
HIGH      >= 85%
CRITICAL  >= 95%
```

Suggested configuration:

```text
ACMS_CONTEXT_ELEVATED_PERCENT=70
ACMS_CONTEXT_HIGH_PERCENT=85
ACMS_CONTEXT_CRITICAL_PERCENT=95
```

Display both ratio and percentage:

```text
137,185 / 200,000 tokens
68.6%
```

If telemetry is null, negative, unparsable, or otherwise invalid, show `UNKNOWN/INVALID`.

Do not infer OOM/context exhaustion merely from an invalid metric.

If the harness/provider explicitly reports context overflow, payload-too-large, or another context failure, create a significant event.

---

## 8. Persistence model

Do not add one durable semantic event for every routine heartbeat.

### Current status

Keep one latest status record per agent containing:

- last heartbeat/contact;
- connectivity;
- session ID/title;
- agent_running;
- model/provider;
- context values;
- assignment correlation;
- warning state.

Update this every heartbeat.

### Telemetry history

Retain periodic samples for trends such as context growth.

Recommended default:

```text
sample every 300 seconds / 5 minutes
```

Also persist immediately on material change:

- context warning level;
- model;
- session;
- `agent_running`;
- connectivity;
- assignment/session alignment.

Exact long-term retention/downsampling may remain future work.

---

## 9. Reconciliation

### Stale

No successful contact for 5 minutes:

```text
Connectivity = STALE
```

Do not alter the assignment.

### One-hour stale reconciliation

If continuously stale for 1 hour, actively request a full status snapshot.

Success:

- update operational telemetry;
- set `HEALTHY`;
- set `last_contact_source=reconciliation`;
- compare ACMS versus harness facts;
- record discrepancies.

Failure:

```text
Connectivity = UNREACHABLE
```

### Fleet reconciliation

Every 24 hours, reconcile every registered agent regardless of status.

Requests may be staggered later if fleet size warrants it.

### Reconciliation authority

When observed harness status disagrees with ACMS:

- correct current operational telemetry automatically;
- preserve history;
- create a discrepancy event;
- leave Work Item scope and Assignment unchanged.

No human approval is required merely to correct observed status.

---

## 10. Session/work alignment

UI should show both:

```text
Expected by ACMS:
  ACMS-WORK-000042
  Implement Agent Bridge heartbeat

Reported by harness:
  Session ID: ...
  Session Title: [ACMS-WORK-000042] Implement Agent Bridge heartbeat
```

Derive:

```text
ALIGNED
UNKNOWN
MISMATCH
```

This is not an A2A task state.

A mismatch may mean:

- structured work key differs;
- session title contains a different ACMS work key;
- assignment key differs;
- agent is actively running but no correlation is reported after assignment delivery.

Use a short grace period after a new assignment/title operation, recommended:

```text
ACMS_SESSION_ALIGNMENT_GRACE_SECONDS=120
```

### Persistent mismatch

After the grace period while the agent is running:

1. create `SESSION_ASSIGNMENT_MISMATCH`;
2. request a structured Markdown handoff if supported;
3. associate the handoff with expected work and observed session;
4. surface it in Agent Detail and Work UI;
5. do not rewrite approved scope.

The handoff should state what the agent believes it is doing, current goal, actions completed, artifacts/files changed, blockers, and next intended action.

Automatic semantic review/redirection by a manager/executive agent can follow later. Implement the data/control primitive now.

---

## 11. Semantic event log

Implement/extend the durable event store required by `ACMS-REQ-035`.

Each significant event should contain as applicable:

```text
event_id
sequence
timestamp
event_type
actor/source
agent_id
work_key
assignment_key
A2A task ID
harness session ID
one-sentence summary
structured metadata
correlation/trace ID
```

Useful event types:

```text
CONNECTIVITY_CHANGED
AGENT_RUNNING_CHANGED
SESSION_CHANGED
MODEL_CHANGED
CONTEXT_WARNING_CHANGED
CONTEXT_TELEMETRY_INVALID
CONTEXT_OVERFLOW
ASSIGNMENT_DISPATCHED
ASSIGNMENT_ACKNOWLEDGED
A2A_TASK_STATE_CHANGED
CONTROL_REQUESTED
CONTROL_ACKNOWLEDGED
CONTROL_FAILED
RECONCILIATION_STARTED
RECONCILIATION_SUCCEEDED
RECONCILIATION_FAILED
SESSION_ASSIGNMENT_MISMATCH
HANDOFF_REQUESTED
HANDOFF_RECEIVED
```

Routine heartbeat receipt itself does not need a semantic event every minute.

Use concise summaries such as:

> Hermes Worker 01 became STALE after five minutes without contact.

---

## 12. A2A/Agent Bridge control contract

Implement a canonical ACMS bridge contract and first Hermes adapter.

Capabilities:

```text
status
send_work
steer
pause
resume
interrupt
cancel
request_handoff
set_session_title
```

Each harness advertises support independently.

### `send_work`

Send:

- work key;
- assignment key;
- scope/instruction;
- desired session title;
- relevant context references;
- correlation ID.

Record timestamps/events for:

```text
recorded by ACMS
dispatch attempted
bridge acknowledged
A2A task created/submitted
```

These are event facts, not substitutes for A2A task state.

### `steer`

Additional direction into the existing active task/session without silently changing approved scope.

### `pause`

Required semantics:

> Temporarily suspend forward execution while preserving the same task/session so `resume` continues it.

Only advertise if tested.

### `resume`

Continue that genuinely paused task/session.

### `interrupt`

Required semantics:

> Stop the current active run/turn while preserving session/context so later direction can continue.

Non-terminal at Work Item level.

### `cancel`

Required semantics:

> Terminally cancel the current A2A execution task.

Continuing the same Work Item afterward requires a new A2A execution task.

---

## 13. Mandatory harness-control research

Do not guess mappings.

Create:

`docs/HARNESS_CONTROL_MAPPING.md`

For each harness record:

| ACMS capability | Required semantics | Harness mapping | Tested version | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| status | full current status | ... | ... | ... |
| send_work | dispatch controlled work | ... | ... | ... |
| steer | inject direction | ... | ... | ... |
| pause | resumable suspension | ... | ... | supported/unsupported |
| resume | resume same paused unit | ... | ... | ... |
| interrupt | stop run, preserve session | ... | ... | ... |
| cancel | terminal task cancellation | ... | ... | ... |
| request_handoff | request Markdown handoff | ... | ... | ... |
| set_session_title | set work-linked title | ... | ... | ... |

If a harness has a meaningful operation that lacks an ACMS mapping, log the gap for architecture review instead of silently ignoring it.

---

## 14. Hermes research baseline and required live tests

Before coding mappings, verify the **actual installed Hermes version**.

Planning research against current upstream `NousResearch/hermes-agent` showed:

- `/status` is centralized around session ID, title, model/provider, created/last activity, token count, and `agent_running`;
- upstream `/stop` explicitly interrupts a running agent while preserving the session so the user can continue;
- `/new` rotates/resets the session and supports `/new <title>`;
- `/platform pause|resume` concerns messaging platform adapters, not necessarily agent execution Pause/Resume;
- upstream has explicit context-overflow/payload-too-large error classifications.

These are research inputs only. Do not assume the installed version behaves identically.

On a non-critical Hermes agent test:

1. record exact version/commit;
2. capture status before execution;
3. dispatch a controlled ACMS-keyed task;
4. verify title and session ID;
5. verify steering;
6. test stop/interrupt and confirm session can continue;
7. research/test true Pause/Resume;
8. test `/new` and confirm new session behavior;
9. determine/test terminal cancel mapping;
10. verify context telemetry sources;
11. record results in `HARNESS_CONTROL_MAPPING.md`.

If true Pause/Resume is absent, advertise unsupported. Never fake Pause with Interrupt.

---

## 15. Hermes Agent Bridge

The bridge should:

- identify bridge and Hermes versions;
- expose A2A-compatible Agent Card/profile;
- advertise supported ACMS extension capabilities;
- emit status heartbeat;
- accept A2A work/messages;
- translate lifecycle controls to tested Hermes behavior;
- report success/failure;
- return/preserve Hermes session ID;
- apply/report session title;
- carry ACMS work/assignment keys;
- answer reconciliation status queries;
- support handoff requests where feasible.

Keep Hermes internals out of ACMS core domain logic.

---

## 16. Scheduling implementation

Implement lightweight ACMS background scheduling for:

- stale evaluation;
- one-hour stale reconciliation;
- daily fleet reconciliation;
- telemetry sampling.

Do not introduce Celery, Kubernetes, or an event bus solely for this.

Document that if ACMS later runs multiple replicas, scheduled jobs will need leader/lock coordination to avoid duplicate reconciliation.

---

## 17. UI changes

### Fleet

Show:

```text
Agent
Connectivity
Agent Running
Expected Work
Reported Session
Alignment
Last Contact
Model
Context usage
Context warning
```

### Agent Detail

Add:

- connectivity;
- last heartbeat/contact source;
- `agent_running`;
- expected Work/Assignment keys;
- actual session ID/title;
- alignment;
- model/provider;
- context used/max/percentage;
- warning state;
- session/cumulative usage when known;
- connected platforms;
- supported controls;
- control buttons;
- recent semantic events;
- reconciliation result/time;
- handoff links.

### Work view

Show:

- Work key;
- Assignment key;
- dispatch/ack timestamps;
- A2A task ID/state;
- bound harness session;
- latest status;
- alignment;
- handoffs.

Do not label a database assignment as delivered until bridge/A2A acknowledgment exists.

### Controls

Only enable advertised capabilities:

```text
Send Work
Steer
Pause
Resume
Interrupt
Cancel
Request Handoff
Reconcile Now
```

Initially restrict mutating controls to Administrator unless a more specific accepted role rule already exists.

---

## 18. Security

Preserve current private-network boundaries.

- authenticate machine/bridge calls;
- keep human UI on LLDAP/session auth;
- never expose the machine bearer token to browsers;
- authorize control actions and audit the acting human/agent;
- do not publicly expose internal Agent Bridges;
- External Agent Gateway remains out of scope.

---

## 19. Database migration

Use add-only/backward-compatible migration where practical.

Likely structures:

```text
work_items
  + work_key

work_assignments
  + assignment_key
  + dispatch/ack/session correlation

agent_status_current
agent_telemetry_samples
agent_events
agent_session_bindings
```

# AGENTS.md

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous agents working in ACMS.

## Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope, approve significant decisions, define mandatory governance boundaries, and approve merges.

Agents may inspect the repository, plan, implement approved requirements, decompose approved scope into tasks, create tests, update affected documentation, create handoffs, add out-of-scope discoveries to `FUTURE_WORK.md`, draft ADRs/TDRs, and execute non-destructive work inside explicitly approved scope/budget/security/environment boundaries.

Agents must not silently add/remove product scope, promote future work, accept their own new significant ADR, merge their own feature work, exceed a budget, cross tenant/context boundaries, deploy to production, alter credentials/permissions, perform destructive infrastructure actions, or modify protected Organization/Product/Project/Feature context without required human authorization.

## Read before planning

For every meaningful change read:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `SPRINT.md`
4. `docs/BUSINESS_PROBLEMS.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. relevant accepted/proposed ADRs
7. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope/follow-up work is involved
8. `INITIAL_IDEAS.md` when source provenance matters

## Requirement discipline

Implementation must trace to one or more `ACMS-REQ-###` entries. Before editing summarize the requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, files/components likely to change, assumptions, and validation.

If work is outside approved requirements, ask for human approval or record it in `FUTURE_WORK.md`.

## Future-work provenance

Every future-work item must identify one of:

- **Human-Directed**
- **Agent-Discovered**
- **Source-Derived**

Human-Directed does not mean approved; it remains future scope until promoted.

## Centralized governance, decentralized execution

ACMS controls approved assignment, scope, budget, context access, permissions, and governance. It must not become the synchronous permission service for every shell command/tool call/test/retry.

A worker agent may autonomously execute implementation details inside its approved envelope.

An agent may have:

- one primary ACMS work assignment; and
- zero or more registered background routines/cron jobs.

## Context authority

Protected context levels:

- Organization
- Product
- Project
- Feature

Agents may propose changes but may not modify these without human authorization or explicit delegated authority.

Agent, Task, and Live Session context may be modified within approved policy.

If approved scope appears wrong, execute it as far as reasonably possible, preserve state, create a handoff, and propose the alternate direction instead of silently changing scope.

## Handoffs

Meaningful work must produce/update a Markdown handoff recording:

1. assigned goal and requirement IDs;
2. what was attempted;
3. what actually changed;
4. what the agent believes the current state is;
5. validation and results;
6. blockers/assumptions/unresolved questions;
7. recommended next action.

## ADR policy

Human-explicit durable architecture decisions may be recorded as `Accepted`. Agent-originated significant decisions remain `Proposed` until human approval.

## Approval timeout policy

- Reversible in-scope decision: checkpoint/preserve state, use documented fallback, continue if safe.
- Consequential but deferable action: defer that action and continue unrelated work.
- Mandatory human gate: never infer approval from timeout; hold/deny the gated action and continue only unrelated authorized work.

## Security

- Never commit credentials/secrets/customer-sensitive data.
- External/customer agents are a separate trust domain.
- Internal Agent Bridge endpoints are private-network/VPN endpoints unless an accepted ADR says otherwise.
- Do not weaken authentication/authorization/audit to simplify implementation.

## Git workflow

The documentation bootstrap is human-authorized for direct import. After bootstrap, feature work must use a dedicated branch and PR.

Recommended branch: `type/ACMS-short-description`.

Recommended commit: `type(scope): short description (ACMS-REQ-###)` plus issue/ticket when available.

Every feature PR must list requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, validation, risks, ADR/TDR changes, future work, and reviewer focus. Agents do not self-merge.

{"content": "1|# ADR-0009: Deterministic safe-release transaction with automated rollback\n2|\n3|**Status:** Proposed\n4|**Date:** 2026-09-26\n5|\n6|## Context\n7|\n8|The feature-delivery plan (2026-09-26) requires rapid, vertical feature slices\n9|deployed to the live internal ACMS on MIAM-00135 CT122. The existing\n10|`update.sh` path records the previous commit but does not create a pre-deploy\n11|database backup, does not isolate writes during migration, and has no\n12|automated rollback: a bad migration would require human diagnosis and manual\n13|repair, which blocks the \"live system becomes more useful after nearly every\n14|deployment\" cadence.\n15|\n16|Production reconnaissance (2026-09-26) confirmed: repo at `/opt/acms/repo`\n17|(detached HEAD `eab493f` = origin/main), Compose project `acms`, no `pg_dump`\n18|on the CT host (must run inside the postgres container), no `curl` in the app\n19|image, nginx allowlist blocks non-VM source IPs, and MARION has no\n20|`home.arpa` DNS (raw IPs are the convention).\n21|\n22|## Decision\n23|\n24|Deployments run through a **safe release transaction** (`deploy/release.sh`):\n25|\n26|1. **Preflight** — target must be an ancestor of approved `origin/main`;\n27|   production tree clean; postgres healthy; current release healthy; disk\n28|   headroom; required secret keys present (names checked, values never\n29|   printed).\n30|2. **Maintenance window** — nginx serves a 503 maintenance page and\n31|   `acms-app` is stopped: no writes between the backup and acceptance.\n32|3. **Verified backup** — `pg_dump -Fc` executed inside the postgres container\n33|   to `/opt/acms/releases/backups/<release-id>.dump`; validated by size floor,\n34|   `PGDMP` magic, and `pg_restore --list` parse; SHA-256 recorded.\n35|4. **Deploy** — checkout the approved SHA, build the image tagged\n36|   `acms-app:<git-sha-short>` with build identity baked in (`ACMS_BUILD_*`),\n37|   `alembic upgrade head`, start the target app.\n38|5. **Two-stage validation** — Stage `app` (direct container checks while\n39|   maintenance is ON), then maintenance OFF and Stage `full` (HTTPS proxy\n40|   path, UI login, auth denials, build-identity match through the proxy).\n41|6. **Accept or auto-rollback** — on any validation failure the transaction\n42|   (still open) authorizes Level 2: app rollback to the previous SHA +\n43|   verified restore of the pre-deploy backup. `deploy/rollback.sh` also\n44|   supports standalone (Level 1, app-only) and `<release-id>` targeting.\n45|\n46|Release state (ledger `history.jsonl`, per-release metadata, backups) lives in\n47|`/opt/acms/releases/` outside Git; secrets never enter release metadata. The\n48|UI System page and `/version` report version + Git SHA + build time (plan §4);\n49|a Git SHA is the authoritative deployed code identity.\n50|\n51|## Consequences\n52|\n53|- **Safer velocity:** every later merge can deploy through the same process;\n54|  a bad release returns to the exact previous state deterministically.\n55|- **Brief internal outage per deploy** (maintenance window) — accepted for MVP\n56|  simplicity over zero-downtime complexity (plan §6).\n57|- **Level-1 rollback limitation:** after a migration, application-only\n58|  rollback runs old app on new schema; safe for additive migrations (plan §24\n59|  expand/contract), dangerous otherwise — hence the transaction guard.\n60|- **No automatic Alembic downgrade, ever** (plan §29); destructive migrations\n61|  require human approval and restore testing (plan §24).\n62|- Operator education: `update.sh` remains as a deprecated wrapper delegating\n63|  to `release.sh`.\n64|\n65|## References\n66|\n67|- Feature-delivery plan 2026-09-26 §3–§11, §24, §28–§30\n68|- ADR-0007 (MVP stack), ADR-0008 (server-rendered UI)\n69|- ACMS-REQ-039 (authenticated/authorized agent control — release tooling is\n70|  operator-side and never bypasses app authn/authz)\n71|- Branch `ops/ACMS-safe-release-rollback`", "total_lines": 70, "file_size": 3706, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}
